# Arbitrary values: every number on mantle's write path, its vendored crates, its bench and its tests

**Question.** The owner's rule: "We have a no ARBITRARY VALUES rule. Learned and derived approaches
or exceedingly well researched ONLY." Every number in mantle must be (a) learned at runtime from
what the running system measures, (b) derived by a stated model from measured inputs or a
structural fact, or (c) backed by exceedingly well-researched literature or primary sources. Her
question beside it: why does a three-node cell handle about 100 small PUTs a second when she
expects about 10,000? This note finds every number that breaks the rule, says what each costs,
and says what each should be learned or derived from.

**How.** Read on 2026-11-12 from origin/lean-doors at 4c6c0dd8, its vendored hyper-raft crates
(vendor/, 0b1a12ac: hyper-raft 294678c), docs/research/71-the-doors-costs.md and its "Step 9,
after" section ("research/71" below), and docs/design/constants.md. Reading and grepping only:
nothing was built or run.

- Every `const` and `static` in `crates/*/src`, `crates/*/tests`, `crates/*/benches`,
  `crates/*/examples` and the vendored crates was extracted with its doc comment (3,596 items),
  joined to constants.md's row where it has one, and kept where it is a number: a numeric type,
  or an array, tuple or struct of numbers. Strings, labels, enum lists and atomics were left out.
  Test code follows `scripts/check-contracts.py`'s rule (an inline `#[cfg(test)]` module, a file
  whose first item is `#![cfg(test)]`), and also a file a `#[cfg(test)] mod x;` declaration
  includes (six engine scenario files and hyper-quic's `src/tests/`, which the gate counts as
  production).
- Numbers that are not `const` items were found by scanning production code for struct-literal
  fields, `Duration` constructors, literal arguments, shifts and comparisons, and by reading the
  write path end to end: `crates/node` (derive, process, node, host, ask, client, listener),
  `crates/protocol/src/bounds.rs`, `crates/gateway`, `crates/range`, `crates/meta` (session,
  wire), `crates/chunk`, `crates/disk` (calibrate, rounds), `crates/client`, and in vendor/
  hyper-log, hyper-durable, hyper-raft, hyper-transport, hyper-quic's configuration, hyper-rt-
  transport, hyper-block's commit and issuer. The bench defaults are `crates/mantle/src/main.rs`'s
  and `bench*.rs`'s.
- The vendored crates on the write path are hyper-log, hyper-durable, hyper-raft, hyper-transport,
  hyper-quic, hyper-rt, hyper-rt-transport, hyper-block, hyper-datagram, hyper-liveness and
  hyper-timing. hyper-tls, hyper-seal, hyper-swim, hyper-sim and hyper-measure (236 numeric
  constants) carry no number a PUT's path runs on and are left out, as are aws-lc-rs and
  aws-lc-sys.
- Each number got one verdict. **Learned**: measured by the running system. **Derived**: computed
  by a stated model from measured inputs or a structural fact; a field width, tag, magic, version
  or unit conversion is a structural fact, and so are bytes a structure allocates when the counting
  allocator counted them and a test pins them (a property of the code, not of a machine).
  **Well-researched**: a specification, RFC, OS header or vendor reference that fixes the value for
  interoperability, or peer-reviewed literature used as it prescribes. **ARBITRARY**: everything
  else, in the rule's six kinds: picked by hand; read off one measured curve by a chosen criterion
  and frozen; copied from another implementation's tuning (RocksDB, SplinterDB, botocore, the AWS
  CLI, quinn, Chromium and quiche, tokio, focal, slates, HDFS, GFS, Tectonic, Centrifuge); tuned to
  one machine; a guessed timer or deadline; a fixed count bounding concurrency where the system
  could learn it. A "floor" (the least that works) taken as the operating value is arbitrary: the
  criterion, least memory, is chosen, and it ignores what the value costs in throughput. A value
  derived from an arbitrary input is arbitrary. A value a stock client or another system states
  about itself, which mantle must meet (a client's read timeout, its part size), is a fact about
  that client, not a tuning of mantle's, and counts as well-researched.
- Impact is on write throughput, latency, memory or faults: **high** where the number caps
  operations a second or the measured critical path names it; **medium** where it sets a cost on
  the path that research/71 measured or the code shows is paid per operation; **low** otherwise.
  Where an impact is not measured the row says so.
- The scripts and their outputs are in `/tmp/constaudit/` on the Mac (`extract2.py`, `gen.py`,
  `emit.py`, the verdict tables `overrides_*.py` and `literals_data.py`), not in the repository.

**Answer.** Of 1,920 numbers, 1 is learned, 968 derived, 350 well-researched and 601 arbitrary;
17 of the arbitrary ones are high impact, and every one of those is a count, a window or a rate on
the write path or in the bench that measures it. A cell does about 100 small PUTs a second because
each stage of a PUT is held to a fixed count: the bench
offers 100 a second and at most 16 clients; a node runs 8 operations, 10 S3 connections and 8
native clients; a range takes 8 commands an entry and keeps 2 appends out; a volume flushes 24
chunks at most; every QUIC connection's window is the 12,000-byte initial congestion window; a
node runs on one core. By Little's law the cell holds at most 24 operations in flight, so at the
93–117 ms a PUT takes alone it cannot pass about 240 a second, and with all three nodes on one
btrfs it reaches the file system's flush rate first: 1.8 flushes an operation on each node, 466
flushes a second for six writers, about 86 PUTs a second, which is what research/71 measured
(50–86). Ten thousand a second at the same latency needs about 1,000 operations in flight in the
cell and about 64 operations' entries and chunks in each flush of each node's log and volume,
where 0.56 go now; no fixed count above admits that, and none of them was derived from the
throughput it limits.

## 1. What holds a cell near 100 small PUTs a second

A PUT's critical path, one client, closed loop (research/71, "Step 9, after"): the client to the
gateway (0.4–0.5 ms), the bucket's row read by a quorum (0.3–0.7 ms), the chunks to three replicas
at once, each a volume write and one `fdatasync` (12–20 ms; 213 ms at 8 MiB), then the Block, File
and Name commands one after another, each a Raft round of frames flushed and confirmed (22–29 ms
each), and the answer (0.3 ms): 117 ms at 1 B, 93 ms at 64 KiB, 379 ms at 8 MiB, seven flushes on
the path. The waits between flushes were under 0.6 ms at the median: the flushes are the latency.
On the box's btrfs a 4 KiB write and `fdatasync` took 5.6–12.5 ms at the median, and the file
system joined concurrent flushes: 124 a second from one writer, 466 from six and 478 from sixteen.

Throughput is then the least of four bounds (Little 1961: λ = L/W for each stage):

| bound | what sets it now | value | throughput it allows | evidence |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| offered load | `mantle bench doors --load open:100`; the closed ladder's top, `FULL_CLIENTS` | 100/s; 16 clients | 100/s open; 16 / W closed (≈ 160/s at W = 0.1 s, less as W grows under load) | main.rs:466, :1121 |
| operations in flight | `derive::OBJECTS` a node (and `gateway.clients`, the S3 listener's `CONNECTIONS`) | 8 a node, 24 a cell (10 S3 connections a node) | 24 / W ≈ 240/s at W = 0.1 s | research/71: 30, 39, 72, 79, 93/s at 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 a node |
| the shared file system's flushes | each node's log one frame at a time, each volume one batch at a time, three nodes on one btrfs; flushes per operation fall only as operations share a flush, which `batch()` (8 commands an entry), `IN_FLIGHT` (2 appends), the volume's 24 chunks a batch and `OBJECTS` bound | 1.8 flushes an operation a node (7.1 at one client); 466 flushes/s at six writers | 466 / (3 × 1.8) ≈ 86/s | research/71: 24 clients, 50–86/s, 1.63 commands an entry, 1.18 entries an append, 1.81 chunks a volume flush |
| CPU | `runtime.shards = 1`: both doors, every range and the transport on one thread a node | one core a node | not measured as the binding bound yet; at research/71's 4.7–6.7 M server instructions a small PUT (three servers summed, before steps 5–7's cuts), 10,000/s asks 16–22 G instructions a second of each node's one core | process.rs:1695; research/71 ledger |

The flush bound is the one the after-build met: 466 flushes a second over 5.4 flushes a PUT (1.8 on
each of three nodes sharing one file system) is 86 a second, the top of the 50–86 measured. The
concurrency bound sits just above it: knee or no knee, 24 operations at 0.1 s is 240 a second, and
under load W grows (p50 4.0 s at 24 clients, the generator's queue included). The bench's 100 a
second is an offered rate: no open-loop phase can show more.

**What 10,000 small PUTs a second asks.** At W ≈ 0.1 s, Little's law asks L ≈ 1,000 operations in
flight in the cell, about 333 a node, against 8 now. On the same file system's 466 flushes a second
it asks 0.047 flushes a PUT in the cell, so each node's flush must carry about 64 operations'
entries and chunks where it carries 0.56 now: a range's entry must take as many commands as are
waiting (8 now), a follower as many appends as cover a round trip and a flush (2 now), a volume's
batch as many chunks as arrived during its last flush (24 now), each connection a window covering
its path's bandwidth-delay product (12,000 bytes now), the S3 door as many connections as the
node's memory holds (10 now), and the node as many cores as it measures (1 now). Each of those is a
fixed count in the code; section 3 ranks them and gives each its model. Two structural limits stand
beside them and are not numbers: each node's log writes one frame at a time (hyper-log's
`PIPELINE_FRAMES`: one on the device, one behind it, one gathering) and a node keeps one entry out
to a range at a time (`ask.rs`, one session, `asking.out`); and the latency floor of seven
sequential flushes, which only the owner's decisions 2 and 3 of research/71 (answering at a frame's
own flush; committing Block, File and Name together) would shorten. Those set W; the counts set L.

The bench measured all three nodes on one btrfs, so the cell's six writers shared one file system's
log commit (one PUT saw all three nodes' flushes end within 30 µs, research/71). A cell of a node a
machine has three file systems' flushes; the bench's own setup is a third of a cell's flush budget.

## 2. Count by verdict

| scope | items | learned | derived | well-researched | ARBITRARY | of which high | medium | low |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| mantle production constants | 759 | 0 | 528 | 155 | 76 | 3 | 20 | 53 |
| mantle production literal parameters (not `const`) | 36 | 0 | 12 | 1 | 23 | 7 | 8 | 8 |
| vendored write-path constants | 579 | 0 | 339 | 167 | 73 | 0 | 7 | 66 |
| vendored literal parameters (not `const`) | 12 | 1 | 1 | 3 | 7 | 1 | 1 | 5 |
| bench and harness constants (crates/mantle/src bench, main, transfer) | 39 | 0 | 12 | 7 | 20 | 4 | 5 | 11 |
| bench and harness literal parameters | 14 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 14 | 2 | 4 | 8 |
| test constants (crates/*/tests and test-only modules) | 481 | 0 | 76 | 17 | 388 | 0 | 0 | 388 |
| **all** | **1920** | **1** | **968** | **350** | **601** | **17** | **45** | **539** |

Rows count numbers: a `const` is one, and a literal parameter is one even where its row names a
few fields set together (calibration's plan, a harness runtime). "Learned" is 1 because the code's
learned values are not literals: they are models whose inputs the running node measures, and none
of them is counted above. On the write path they are the device profile (the read depth, read
bytes, read gap and prewrite a volume runs with, `mantle_disk::calibrate`, measured at a node's
first start and kept while the device matches), the runtime's calibration (`hyper_rt::machine`:
wake and system-call costs, the spin threshold, Wilks' 59 samples a probe), the memory allowance
(cgroup `memory.max`, `RLIMIT_AS`, physical memory), each path's RTT, PTO and congestion window
(hyper-quic, RFC 9002 §5–§7), hyper-log's gather wait (`hyper_block::commit::Anticipation`), and
hyper-durable's patience (an RFC 6298 fold of the backlog's growth). Those are the shape every
count in section 3 should take.

Of the 601 arbitrary numbers, 388 are tests' constants (frozen configurations, picked counts and
guessed waits, section 4.3), 34 are the bench's and the harness's, and 179 are production code
mantle runs: 99 in mantle (76 constants, 23 literal parameters) and 80 in the vendored crates (73
constants, 7 literal parameters). Of mantle's 99, constants.md marks 5 open. It labels 37 cited
(another implementation's default, a practice or an anecdote), 13 derived (from an arbitrary input,
or from a premise that does not hold), 10 bound (picked) and 8 measured (once, on one machine), and
26 are not in it at all: 3 constants of struct or array type and the 23 literal parameters, which
`scripts/check-contracts.py` does not see (section 5). The vendored crates' numbers have no
inventory in mantle.

## 3. The arbitrary values on the write path, ranked

Ranked by what they cap: first the counts that bound operations in flight and operations a flush
(each a factor of λ = L/W or of flushes a PUT), then the windows and timers that set W, then the
costs per operation. Every replacement keeps the memory bound the count stood in for: a learned
limit is capped above by what the node's allowance past its floor holds of the thing it admits
(`derive::floor`, `derive::objects`), so CLAUDE.md §2 still holds.

| # | where | value | what it caps | basis claimed | why it is arbitrary | impact, with evidence | replacement: what to learn or derive, from which inputs, by which model |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:152` `OBJECTS` | 8 | operations a node runs at once, of any kind; one past it is answered `SlowDown` (host.rs:652) | "the knee of the cell's write throughput measured on the box" (constants.md: measured) | read off one curve (n 300, one machine, a busy host) by a criterion chosen there, "the doubling past 8 adding 10%, inside the 13–30% run-to-run band", then frozen. The curve had not flattened: 72, 79 and 93/s at 8, 16 and 32 | **high**: L ≤ 24 in a cell, λ ≤ 24 / W ≈ 240/s at 0.1 s; before step 9 (two a node, one command an entry) the cell stopped at 20–47/s, after it 50–86/s (research/71) | a concurrency limit learned at runtime per node, as TCP Vegas learns a window (Brakmo and Peterson, SIGCOMM 1994): with baseRTT the least operation latency measured and RTT the current one, raise the limit while `limit · (1 − baseRTT/RTT)`, the operations queued, is under α and lower it past β; or Breakwater's credits against a measured queueing-delay target (Cho et al., OSDI 2020, which hyper-transport's pool already cites); Little's law says the limit settles at λ·W; capped above by the allowance past the floor at `derive::objects`'s bytes an operation, below by one |
| 2 | `crates/node/src/process.rs:1695` `runtime.shards` | 1 | cores the node's loop runs on: both doors, every range, the transport | node.md §1.2, "the node runs its ranges on one shard" | a fixed count of one core whatever the host measures (`cores` is measured and said at start, then not used) | **high**: CPU per small PUT 4.7–6.7 M server instructions (research/71 ledger, three servers summed), so 10,000/s asks 16–22 G instructions a second of each node's one core; not yet the measured bound | shards from the measured cores (`hyper_rt::machine::facts`, as `RuntimeConfig::from_calibration` already derives) less those the device threads take; ranges placed on shards by their measured load; a lane a shard (`lanes_per_peer`, section 6) |
| 3 | `crates/node/src/listener/mod.rs:44` `CONNECTIONS` (with `REFUSING`, `TASKS`, `BACKLOG`) | 10 (10, 20, 10) | S3 connections a node serves at once, the kernel's accept queue | "what stock clients open to an endpoint by default", botocore's `max_pool_connections` and the AWS CLI's `max_concurrent_requests` (constants.md: cited) | one client's default copied as the server's whole admission: a node serves ten connections whoever and however many its clients are | **high**: the HTTP door holds ≤ 30 requests a cell, ≤ 300/s at 0.1 s; the bench's clients are cut to fit it (`FULL_CLIENTS`, `SLOPE_HIGH`) | admission by the learned concurrency limit of #1, and connections by memory: what the allowance past the floor holds at a connection's measured bytes (TLS state, read buffer, body pieces); the accept queue by Little's law, the measured accept rate times the wait for a slot; keep the `503 SlowDown` refusal past it |
| 4 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:419` `batch()` → `rules.max_answers` | `objects × window` = 8 | commands one entry of a node's carries, and the answers a session keeps | derived (`gateway.objects × gateway.window`) | inherits #1 and #10: a count of operations stands in for what is waiting | **high**: under load 1.63 commands an entry (at most 6) and 1.18 entries an append (research/71): a range commits at most 8 of a node's commands a Raft round | an entry takes every command waiting when it goes, bounded by its bytes (`max_entry_bytes`) and the session's answers by the row S3 admits (`wire::largest_row_bytes`); what is waiting is bounded by #1's learned limit. Group commit's rule that one write and flush commits the group (Helland et al., Tandem TR 88.1, §2; DeWitt et al., SIGMOD 1984, §5.2) sets no count |
| 5 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:414` `IN_FLIGHT` | 2 | appends a leader keeps out to each follower, `max_inflight_msgs` | derived: "the log's one frame on the device and one queued behind it: a third waits for the second either way" | the premise counts frames; the bound counts appends. A follower's frame gathers every append that arrived while its last flushed, so a third append out joins the second's frame instead of waiting a round trip to be sent | **high**: entries a range has in flight to a follower; with #4, at most 2 × 8 of a node's commands in a follower's pipeline | appends out = the follower's measured append rate × (round trip + its flush), Little's law, re-learned per follower, as Raft's pipelining leaves to the leader (Ongaro 2014 §10.2.1; Santos and Schiper, ICDCN 2012, derive the window and batch from measured bandwidth, latency and CPU); bytes stay bounded by `max_uncommitted_size` |
| 6 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:1318` volume `batch_requests` | identities × batch = 24 (queue 48) | chunks one volume group commit holds; past the queue a chunk is answered `Busy` | derived from #4 | inherits #1: a count of operations | **high**: 1.81 chunks a volume flush under load (research/71); at most 24, whatever arrived | a batch is what arrived while the last flush ran (the writer already gathers so), bounded by the segment's room in bytes (`batch_bytes`) and the queue by memory, not by a count of operations |
| 7 | `crates/protocol/src/bounds.rs:167` `least_transport`, and hyper-transport's `transport()` windows (`vendor/hyper-transport/src/endpoint.rs:231`) | each share at `initial_window(1,200)` = 12,000 B | every QUIC connection's flow-control window, the transport's budget | "the transport's floor ... each share at QUIC's initial window" (node.md §1.4) | RFC 9002 §7.2's initial *congestion* window used as every connection's *flow-control* ceiling; a floor taken as the operating value | **high**: a chunk's trip to a replica took 47 ms at it and 13 ms at 1–4 MiB; the overload `SlowDown`s were exchanges `Closed` while their bodies waited on the transfer class's window (research/71) | each connection's window = its path's measured bandwidth-delay product, BBR's BtlBw × RTprop (Cardwell et al., ACM Queue 2016), as receive autotuning does (Semke, Mahdavi and Mathis, SIGCOMM 1998; Fisk and Feng's dynamic right-sizing, 2001); RFC 9000 §4 leaves the size to the receiver; summed within the node's memory share. The client-body regression research/71 saw at 1–4 MiB is a loss behaviour to fix in hyper-quic, not a reason for the floor |
| 8 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:173` `gateway.clients` | = OBJECTS = 8 | native-protocol client identities a node admits | derived | inherits #1 | **high**: the native door's 24 clients a cell; the bench's native phases are cut to it (`native_most`) | the transport's identities by memory (a connection's measured bytes) and the operations they run by #1 |
| 9 | `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:473` the durable-write job's `depth` | 1 | the flush concurrency the device profile measures | none stated | a fixed count where the system could learn the curve: the profile measures flushes one at a time only | **high**: research/71 measured this btrfs at 124 flushes/s from one writer and 466–478 from 6–16; nothing in a node learns that, so nothing can decide how many independent logs and volume writers a device should carry | a durable-write ladder by depth like the read ladder (depth up while flushes a second grow past their interval), whose knee (Kleinrock's power, which `calibrate::knee` already uses) gives the writers a device carries |
| 10 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:171` `gateway.window` | 1 | blocks a PUT holds going down and a GET holds reading | "a window of one block, the floor" | a floor taken as the operating value | **high** for objects past one block, none for small ones: an 8 MiB+ PUT's second block waits for the first's Block command, about 17 ms (research/71) | blocks in flight = the measured block-commit latency × the client's measured body rate / the block (Little's law), within the memory an operation may hold |
| 11 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:469` `memtable_bytes` | one row at the range's bounds (≈ 20 KB) | how much an engine buffers before it packs into its trunk | "the least write buffer, one row at the bounds whole" | a floor | **medium, not measured**: at the 1,841 store bytes an object the Block range measured (`STORE_BYTES_AN_OBJECT`), a pack about every dozen small objects; engine and range were 18–34% of a 1 B PUT's instructions (research/71) | size the write buffer against the cache by marginal benefit, the tuner the engine already runs between its regions (Luo and Carey, PVLDB 2021 §5.4), within the engine's share |
| 12 | `crates/gateway/src/layout.rs:13` `CHUNK` | 8 MiB | the largest chunk, so a block's size, the bytes an operation holds, the transfer to each replica | "Tectonic's 'typically 8 MiB' chunk" (cited) | copied from another system's design; constants.md itself notes measured sizing will replace it | **medium**: memory an operation holds (`derive::objects`), so with #1's memory cap the operations a node can run; an 8 MiB chunk took 213 ms to three replicas | the chunk at which a write reaches the device's measured sequential bandwidth (calibration's `sequential_write` saturation) and a transfer's round trip is amortized (the path's bandwidth-delay product), within the memory an operation may hold; per upload where the client states its length |
| 13 | `crates/node/src/derive.rs:46` `FANOUT` | 8 | pivots a trunk node holds before it splits | "SplinterDB's 8" (cited) | another implementation's tuning; constants.md says it waits on a measurement | **medium, not measured**: the trunk's compaction work a row | the pivots one index page holds at the measured pivot bytes, or A/B fanouts as variants by measured compaction cost a row (Conway et al., ATC 2020; Dayan and Idreos, SIGMOD 2018) |
| 14 | `vendor/hyper-quic/src/config/transport.rs:581` `AckFrequencyConfig::decimation` | an ACK every 10 packets, RTT/4, from the 100th | how often a peer acknowledges | "Google QUIC's acknowledgement decimation (quiche, quic_constants.h)" | another implementation's tuning | **medium**: acknowledgement cost and the sender's loss detection on every connection (research/71: ACK handling 4.7–7.5% of servers' instructions at 8–64 MiB) | the sender asks for what its congestion window needs (draft-ietf-quic-ack-frequency leaves the threshold and delay to the sender), from the measured cost of an ACK against its measured effect on loss detection and the window's growth |
| 15 | `vendor/hyper-quic/src/connection/paths.rs:24` `PROBE_VARIANCE_MULTIPLIER` | 2 | the RTT variation's weight in the probe timeout | "Chromium's kPtoRttvarMultiplier, where RFC 9002 §6.2.1 has 4" | another implementation's tuning against the RFC | **medium**: every PTO, and through `bounds::measured_pto` the node's redial backoff and a command's patience | RFC 9002's 4, or learn the weight that holds the measured spurious-PTO rate to a stated target |
| 16 | `vendor/hyper-quic/src/connection/pacing.rs:266-272` `BURST_INTERVAL_NANOS`, `MIN_BURST_SIZE`, `MAX_BURST_SIZE` | 2 ms, 10, 256 | the pacer's burst | quinn: "2ms is chosen", "Creating 256 packets took 1ms in a benchmark" | chosen; tuned to one machine | **medium** for chunk and body transfers | the burst interval from the measured timer lateness (hyper-timing's `Lateness`), the burst from the packets the measured send path builds in that interval, at least the initial window's ten (RFC 9002 §7.2) |
| 17 | `vendor/hyper-transport/src/credit.rs:62,71` `AUTOTUNE_ROUND_TRIPS`, `AUTOTUNE_GROWTH`; `vendor/hyper-quic/src/connection/assembler.rs:388` `MAX_CHUNKS` | 2, 2; 1,024 | when a window grows and by how much; the stream window ceiling (2 · 1,024 · 1,134 B ≈ 2.3 MB) | Chromium QUIC's auto-tuning (via slates); quinn's assembler bound | copied | **medium** once #7 is lifted (today the 12 kB ceiling binds first) | grow to the measured bandwidth-delay product (#7's model) rather than by doubling on a copied trigger; spans from the window over the least stream bytes a packet carries |
| 18 | `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:77,105-115` `Rounds::STANDARD`, `Plan::standard` | rounds 3–6 at ±5%; span 256 MiB; 500 ms rounds; read depths 1, 4, 16, 64 then ×4; sequential 1, 4; 1 MiB; 64 durable writes; 32 MiB | the device profile the issuer's depth, the reads' depth, bytes and gap come from | rounds: research 11 §13.3, "±5%, half the ±10% that comparisons resolve"; the plan: none | the precision is an asserted resolution (research/71's A/A bands were 13–953%); the span, steps, ladder and sizes are picked; a round is a time where measurement.md §5a decides it is a count | **medium**: every volume's issuer depth and read depth | rounds by the precision the decision needs (two depths differ beyond their intervals) with Le Boudec's counts for the quantile used; the span past the measured device cache; bisect the ladder to the knee; grow transfer sizes until throughput stops growing |
| 19 | `crates/meta/src/collector.rs:16` `GRACE_NS` | 3 days | how long a released chunk is kept before it is freed | "GFS keeps deleted files for three days" (cited) | copied from another system | **medium**: nothing a run frees comes back within it; volumes fill (the bench's 32 GiB `FULL_VOLUME` is sized for it), and a full volume refuses writes | the longest a reader may still hold a released chunk (the measured p99.9 of a GET's span, a lease's term) plus the recovery-point objective its operator states (research/22 §10.4) |
| 20 | `crates/client/src/lib.rs:77-93` `ATTEMPTS`, `QUOTA`, `SLOWED_RETRY`, `UNRETRIED_REFUND`, `SLOWED_BASE`, `SLOWED_MOST` | 3; 500, 5, 1; 1 s, 20 s | how often and how long the native client asks again after `SlowDown` | botocore's standard retry mode (cited) | another implementation's tuning | **medium**: research/71's "failed" counts are PUTs refused three times; the client's retries are the load that follows an overload | the server says how long to wait, its measured queue's drain (RFC 9110 `Retry-After`), and the client keeps a token bucket learned from its own refusal rate (research/77's token bucket; the AWS SDKs' adaptive retry mode, which paces by a CUBIC-learned rate from throttling responses, AWS SDKs and Tools Reference Guide); attempts while the deadline and the bucket allow |

The rest of the write path's arbitrary numbers, in the appendix with their replacements:
`derive::STORE_BYTES_AN_OBJECT` (measured once on an M5 Max to 30,000 objects, the slope still
growing, sizes the engines' memory), the durability model's inputs (`REPAIR_HOURS` from a talk,
`POWER_LOSSES` unverified at its source, `MOST_COPIES` and `mantle_ec::CODES` from deployed
systems' practice), which choose a block's scheme and so the bytes a PUT writes; `mantle_s3::seal::SEGMENT`
(GFS's 64 KB), `Gateway.scan_rows` (a floor), `lanes_per_peer` (one, from one shard),
`EXTENT_PAGES` (a floor), hyper-rt-transport's `TURNS` (tokio's 128), `put::RENEWALS`
(Centrifuge's), `derive::HANDOVER` (HDFS's 60 s), `ELECTION_TICK` and `HEARTBEAT_TICK` (focal's,
deciding nothing since ranges elect by suspicion), the engine's RocksDB and codec tunings, and
hyper-quic's quinn defaults (most overridden or unused by mantle, so of no effect: the appendix
says which).

One more on the path is learned, not arbitrary, and still costs: hyper-log's gather wait
(`Waits::Measured`, `hyper_block::commit::Anticipation`) learns a batch's service time and the
share of answered submitters that return, with RFC 6298's gain of 1/8. Its own module states the
condition it is exact under, submitters returning well within a flush; a Raft group's next entry
returns only after a replication round, and research/71 found the gather waited to its deadline in
nearly every gather (p90 6–23 ms a command at one client, 4–8% of the log's time at six). The
module names the general rule, which weighs each returner over the measured distribution of return
times (Iyer and Druschel, SOSP 2001 §3.3). It also counts as a returner any submission that
arrives during the wait (`returned` is the batch's growth since the answers went out), whichever
group sent it. Building the general rule, per group, and counting only the answered groups'
resubmissions as returns, is the replacement.

## 4. The bench's and the harness's arbitrary defaults

### 4.1 `mantle bench doors`

| default | where | value | what it decides | why it is arbitrary | how it should be derived |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--load` | main.rs:466 | `open:100` | the arrival rate of every open-loop phase | hand-set: an open-loop phase cannot report more than 100/s, the number the owner saw | from the run's own measured capacity: the closed ladder's best step μ in the same run, then open-loop rates at fractions ρ·μ where the tail is reported and the rate at which p99.9 meets its objective (Schroeder, Wierman and Harchol-Balter, NSDI 2006, on open versus closed load); the Poisson arrivals stay |
| `--clients` | main.rs:1121 `FULL_CLIENTS` | 16 | open loop's clients; the closed ladder's top | "at most six S3 connections on each of three nodes, within a listener's budget of ten" (bound): inherits `CONNECTIONS` | from Little's law: the clients a closed loop needs to offer λ at the measured W (L = λ·W), the ladder going on while throughput grows beyond its measured interval, bounded by the harness's measured capacity (its cores and memory per logical client), not by the server's admission |
| `--ops` | main.rs:1124 `FULL_OPS` | 368 | operations a phase's step asks | Le Boudec's count for a p99 at 95% (cited), but the plan's bar is p99.9 under load, which needs 3,688 (measurement.md §5a): research/71's cells have no p99.9. measurement.md §5a's own rule is not applied either: `max(⌈fewest(q)/10⌉, ⌈N/(2·5%)⌉)` transfers a round and a dimensioning round's cv raising it to `(1.96·cv/5%)²` (Jain) | the count from the quantile reported (3,688 behind a p99.9) and the dimensioning round's measured cv, as measurement.md §5a states |
| `--bytes` | main.rs:1127 `STEP_BYTES` | 256 MiB | object bytes a step moves at most | picked so a full run fits `FULL_VOLUME` (bound) | bytes a step = the operations the quantile needs × the size, refused where the volume's measured free space past its reserve cannot hold them |
| `--objects` | main.rs:1118 `FULL_OBJECTS` | 100,000 | the small-object set listed and read | "the largest small set of the scale the owner agreed" (cited): an owner's pick is not a derivation | from what the set must exercise: enough objects that each range's rows pass its memtable and cache and each volume's index passes its share (`derive::engine_wants_for`, `index_wants`, with a learned bytes an object), and at least the operations the quantile needs |
| `--volume` | main.rs:1141 `FULL_VOLUME` | 32 GiB | each founder's preallocated volume | derived from the run's bytes, on assumed inputs: a 4 KiB block (the device's is measured), a 1.6% packing loss (the volume reports its own), three attempts each (`ATTEMPTS`, copied), nothing freed (`GRACE_NS`, copied) | the run's planned bytes with the measured block and packing, or `--volume free` less the reserve, as `bench transfer` does |
| closed ladder | bench_doors.rs:95 `GROWTH`; :2732 doubling | stop when a doubling adds < 10%; ×2 | where the ladder stops and which client counts it measures | "the ±10% the benchmarks' repeated runs resolve" (research 11), an asserted resolution where this run's A/A bands were 13–953% | go on while a step's throughput exceeds the last beyond their measured intervals (a rank test on the per-operation samples, or the A/A band measured in the same run); bisect between the last growing and the first flat step |
| sizes | main.rs:1114 `FULL_SIZES` | 0, 1, 65,535, 65,536, 65,537, 128 KiB, 8 MiB, 64 MiB | the sizes measured | the boundaries are structural (a segment either side, S3's 128 KiB line); 8 MiB and 64 MiB are the stock clients' part and "a large object" | keep the boundaries; take the large sizes from what the node derives (one chunk, a block's width of chunks, past a block) |
| slope | bench_doors.rs:103-104 `SLOPE_LOW`, `SLOPE_HIGH` | 6, 24 connections | the two points the per-connection slope is read between | from the listener's ten (inherits `CONNECTIONS`) | the ladder's own lowest and knee steps |
| ETag checks | bench_doors.rs:90 `ETAGS` | 16 keys a phase | which objects' ETags are checked | picked to bound a table | check every object streaming, its MD5 made as the generator makes the bytes, holding nothing that grows |
| harness runtime | bench_doors.rs:3066 | one shard, `ring_entries` 1, `step_budget_ns` 1 s, `timer_tick_ns` 100 µs, `batch` 1, `spin_ns` 0 | the harness's capacity and its arrivals' timing | hand-set: one thread drives every client and checks every byte; at 10,000/s an arrival every 100 µs equals the timer tick; research/71's tails include requests delayed by the harness's own thread | `RuntimeConfig::from_calibration` on the host's measured cores and timer, as the node does, with shards from cores |
| retries | bench_doors.rs:363-364 `SLOWED_BASE_NS`, `SLOWED_MOST_NS`; mantle-client's `ATTEMPTS` | 1 s, 20 s; 3 | how the harness's clients wait after `SlowDown`, and when a PUT counts failed | botocore's (copied) | as #20 in section 3 |
| cell | transfer/cell.rs:23 `FOUNDERS` on one directory | three nodes on one file system | the cell's flush budget | three founders for three copies is well-founded; placing all three on one btrfs makes the cell's six writers share one file system's commit | measure a cell of a node a device (or a machine), as a cell runs; when one machine must serve, state its share of the device's measured flush budget beside every result |

### 4.2 The other benches

`mantle bench chunk`, `log`, `ec`, `gateway`, `hash`, `http`, `s3` and `meta` run for a number of
seconds (1.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 2.0, 0.5 by default, main.rs:273–385) where
measurement.md §5a decides "a round is a count of transfers, not a length of time"; their points
are picked lists (chunk sizes 4K, 64K, 1M, 8M and workers 1, 4, 16, 64; log entries 128, 1K, 16K
and replicas 1, 4, 16, 64, 256; ec, gateway and hash sizes); `bench chunk` runs on the chunk
store's `Config::default` and `Limits::default` (256 MiB segments, 1,024 requests and 32 MiB a
batch, 2^22 fragments), all picked, where `derive::volume` derives each from the measured device;
`bench log` compacts every 64 entries (`KEEP`, open) where the node derives its checkpoint
cadence; `bench meta` keeps 256 answers a session (`HISTORY`, open) where the node derives
`max_answers`; `bench hash` measures 16 MiB bodies (`CHUNKED_BODY`, `FORM_FILE`, open). Each should
take its points from what the node derives (entry, chunk, segment and block sizes; the derived
batch and cadence) and its counts from measurement.md §5a, with ladders for concurrency. Section 6
and appendix A.3 list each.

### 4.3 Tests

388 of the 481 test constants are arbitrary (appendix B). Their patterns, each against CLAUDE.md
rule 3 and the live-tests rule:

- **Frozen configurations.** `crates/mantle/tests/support/cell.rs`'s `STATED` (range settings of
  1,024-byte messages and entries, 2 appends out, `rules.max_answers` 16, `log.segment_bytes`
  65,536, 64 segments, `queue_submissions` 16, an engine of 4-page extents, fanout 3, 96 leaf
  entries, `probe_budget_ms` 40), `crates/node/tests/cluster.rs`'s and `support/cell.rs`'s
  `SETTINGS`, `RULES`, `STORE`, `TRUNK`, `crates/range/tests/*`'s settings, and 143 engine test
  configurations (`CONFIG`, `TRUNK`, `STORE`, `MEM`, `BOUND`, `SLICE_NS`). The cell tests run
  `mantle node`, which is told every number, so they never run the derivation `mantle serve` runs;
  a defect of a derived value cannot show in them. Replace each with the production derivation
  on the host (`derive::cell`, `derive::log`, `derive::engine`) or state it as the boundary the
  test exercises.
- **Guessed waits.** `WAIT` and `WATCH` of 5 s in thirteen engine tests and test modules; 30 s deadlines in
  `crates/chunk/tests/corruption.rs:274` and `tests/common/mod.rs:54` (the second copied from the
  first); `PATIENCE` 40 and `PATIENT_ROUNDS` 40 election ticks in the range simulations; a 3,600 s
  progress bound in `crates/node/tests/transport_*.rs`. Replace with a wait on the fact the test
  needs and no deadline but the run's own.
- **Picked counts and rates.** `SEEDS` 2 in `crates/node/tests/sim.rs` (a sample count), `LOSS_PPM`
  2% and `PROFILES` (focal's path model, copied) in `crates/node/tests/support/cell.rs`,
  `CHECK_BUDGET` 10,000,000 (the range simulation's, copied), `WRITES` 200, `OPS_EACH` 20,
  `MAX_OPS` 2^12, and the engine tests' `ROWS`, `KEYS`, `OPS`, `N`, `DEPTH`. Replace with the count
  the property needs: a boundary crossed, a bound passed, a seed count from Wilks (59 for a 95/95
  bound) where a failure rate is claimed.

## 5. constants.md's labels against the rule

`scripts/check-contracts.py` checks that each numeric `const` in `crates/*/src` has a row with a
kind and that open rows do not pass nine. It cannot check the rule, for three reasons:

- **It does not see most of the numbers that cap throughput.** It reads `const` items of numeric
  types only. `gateway.window: 1`, `scan_rows: 1`, `batch()`, the volume's `batch_requests`,
  `runtime.shards = 1`, `least_transport`'s window, `lanes_per_peer: 1`, the calibration plan and
  `Rounds::STANDARD` (a struct), the bench's `--load open:100` (a clap default) and every vendored
  number are outside it. Of the 17 high-impact arbitrary numbers, 7 are `const` items it sees
  (`OBJECTS`, `IN_FLIGHT`, `CONNECTIONS` and the bench's `FULL_OBJECTS`, `FULL_CLIENTS`,
  `FULL_OPS`, `GROWTH`), each with a row whose kind it accepts.
- **A label is not a basis.** It accepts any kind. The rows below carry a kind the rule does not
  accept as a basis:
  - *measured once, on one machine, frozen*: `derive::OBJECTS`, `STORE_BYTES_AN_OBJECT`,
    `shard_db::ORDER_BOUND`, `memtable::btree::DEGREE`, `zstd::decoder::WILD_MAX`,
    `port::mmap::PAGE_OFFSET`, `store::RANGE_FILTER_BITS`, `RANGE_FILTER_DEPTH` (read off a curve),
    `transfer::place::READER_PIECES` (what passed on one CI runner). The measured rows that count a
    structure's bytes (`MEMBER_BYTES`, `PLANE_PEER_BYTES`, the transport's, the index's, the
    coding's) are properties of the code and hold.
  - *cited, but the citation is another implementation's tuning or practice*: `CONNECTIONS`,
    `ATTEMPTS`, `QUOTA`, `SLOWED_*` (botocore, the AWS CLI); `CHUNK` (Tectonic); `FANOUT`
    (SplinterDB); `HANDOVER` (HDFS); `GRACE_NS`, `seal::SEGMENT` (GFS); `RENEWALS` (Centrifuge);
    `ELECTION_TICK`, `HEARTBEAT_TICK` (focal); `SCRUB` (field practice); `REPAIR_HOURS` (a talk);
    `POWER_LOSSES` (unverified at its source); `MOST_COPIES` (practice); `BITS_PER_KEY`,
    `DEFAULT_WRITE_BUFFER_SIZE`, `MAX_BUFFER_SIZE`, the arena's and skip list's sizes,
    `GUARD_LEN`, `MAX_POOR_SEARCHES`, `WAL_LEVEL` (RocksDB); the codecs' table sizes (zlib, lz4,
    snappy); `GROWTH` (an asserted resolution); `FULL_OBJECTS` (the owner's pick); `FULL_OPS`
    (p99's count where the bar is p99.9).
  - *derived from an arbitrary input or a premise that does not hold*: `IN_FLIGHT` (frames for
    appends), `REFUSING`, `TASKS`, `BACKLOG` (from `CONNECTIONS`), `FULL_VOLUME` (assumed inputs),
    the disk histogram's five derived rows (from two open ones), `PROBES` (from `BITS_PER_KEY`),
    `SEALED`, `SEALED_SEGMENT`, `PLAIN_SEGMENT` (from `SEGMENT`), `FRAME_PAYLOAD` (from
    `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`).
  - *bound, picked*: `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`, `MAX_DAMAGED`, `SEQUENCE_RESERVE`, `INCARNATION_RESERVE`,
    `probe::linux::MAX_DEPTH`, `probe::macos::MAX_STRING`, `MAX_SAVE_POINTS`, `LEAF_INDEX_DEPTH`,
    `SHAPE_DEPTHS`, `EXTENT_PAGES` (a floor), and in the bench `ETAGS`, `SLOPE_LOW`, `SLOPE_HIGH`,
    `FULL_CLIENTS`, `STEP_BYTES`, `SETTLE`.
- **Values drift from their rows.** The gate checks that a row names a constant, not its value.
  Three rows state a value the code no longer has: `crates/chunk/src/index.rs` `FRAGMENT_BYTES`
  (row 346, code 352), `INDEX_BYTES` (row 146, code 180), and `crates/node/src/derive.rs`
  `MEMBER_BYTES` (row 1,584, code 1,672).

What the gate would need to enforce the rule: a row for every literal parameter that sets a count,
window, size, rate, timer or sample count (not only `const` items), in mantle and in vendor/; a
kind that names the model and its measured inputs for every throughput-bearing value; "measured"
split into a structure's bytes (allowed) and a machine's performance (refused); and each row's
value checked against the code's.

## 6. Literal parameters outside `const`

Every number below sets a count, window, size, rate or timer without being a `const` item, so
constants.md has no row for it and the gate never sees it. Ordered arbitrary first, by impact.

| location | parameter | value | what it bounds or decides | basis claimed | verdict | impact | why, and the replacement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:473` | `durable_write job depth` | 1 | flush concurrency measured | — | ARBITRARY | high | the device's flush rate is only ever measured one at a time; research/71 measured this btrfs at 124 flushes/s alone and 466–478 with 6–16 writers. Nothing learns the flush-concurrency curve that sets how many independent logs and volume writers a device should run. Replace: a durable-write ladder by depth like the read ladder |
| `crates/mantle/src/bench_doors.rs:3066` | `harness runtime` | shards 1, ring_entries 1, step_budget 1 s, timer_tick 100 µs, batch 1, spin 0 | the harness's own capacity and arrival-time resolution | — | ARBITRARY | high | hand-set: one thread drives every client; at 10,000/s an arrival every 100 µs equals the timer tick, and research/71's tails include the harness starved by busy loops. Replace: RuntimeConfig::from_calibration on the host's measured cores and timer (as the node does), shards from cores |
| `crates/mantle/src/main.rs:466` | `bench doors --load` | open:100 | offered load of every open-loop phase | — | ARBITRARY | high | a hand-set rate: an open-loop phase can never show more than 100/s. Replace: rates from the run's own measured capacity (the closed ladder's best step): ρ·μ at ρ ∈ fractions where the tail is reported, and the rate where p99.9 meets the objective (Schroeder, Wierman and Harchol-Balter, NSDI 2006) |
| `crates/node/src/derive.rs:1318` | `chunk_requests (volume batch_requests)` | identities × batch = 24 | chunks one volume group commit holds; its queue is twice that | derived from batch | ARBITRARY | high | inherits OBJECTS: at most 24 chunks share a volume flush, the queue past 48 answers Busy. Replace: a batch is what arrived while the last flush ran (the writer already gathers so), bounded by bytes (segment room) and memory, not by a count of operations |
| `crates/node/src/derive.rs:171` | `Gateway.window` | 1 | blocks a PUT holds going down and a GET holds reading | 'a window of one block, the floor' (node.md §1.4) | ARBITRARY | high | a floor taken as the operating value; an 8 MiB+ object's next block waits a Block command (~17 ms, research/71). Replace: blocks in flight = measured block commit latency × the client's measured body rate / block size (Little), within the memory each operation may hold |
| `crates/node/src/derive.rs:173` | `Gateway.clients` | = objects (8) | native-protocol client identities a node admits at once | OBJECTS | ARBITRARY | high | inherits OBJECTS: the native door admits 8 clients a node, 24 a cell. Replace: admit by the learned concurrency limit and the transport's memory per identity |
| `crates/node/src/derive.rs:419` | `batch()` | objects × window = 8 | commands an entry carries; rules.max_answers | derived from OBJECTS and window | ARBITRARY | high | inherits OBJECTS and window: a range commits at most 8 commands an entry from each node (1.63 an entry measured under load, ≤ 6). Replace: commands an entry = the commands waiting when the entry goes (no count bound but the entry's bytes and the session row's answers), the waiting set itself bounded by the learned concurrency limit |
| `crates/node/src/process.rs:1695` | `runtime.shards` | 1 | cores the node's event loop runs on: both doors, every range, the transport | node.md §1.2: 'the node runs its ranges on one shard' | ARBITRARY | high | a fixed count of one core whatever the host measures: a 1 B PUT costs 4.7–6.7 M server instructions (research/71 ledger, three servers summed), so 10,000 PUTs/s asks 16–22 G instructions a second of each node's one core. Replace: shards from the measured cores (hyper-rt Facts::cores, as RuntimeConfig::from_calibration derives), ranges and connections placed across them |
| `crates/protocol/src/bounds.rs:167` | `least_transport (window_ceiling)` | initial_window(1,200) = 12,000 B a share | every connection's flow-control window and the transport's budget | 'the transport at its floor' | ARBITRARY | high | QUIC's initial congestion window (RFC 9002 §7.2) used as the flow-control ceiling of every connection: a chunk's trip to a replica took 47 ms at it against 13 ms at 1–4 MiB, and SlowDowns under load came from exchanges stalled on it (research/71). Replace: window = measured bandwidth-delay product of the path (BBR's BtlBw × RTprop; RFC 9000 §4 leaves sizing to the receiver; Fisk and Feng's dynamic right-sizing), each connection's within the node's memory share |
| `vendor/hyper-transport/src/endpoint.rs:231-249` | `transport(): receive_window / stream window / send_window` | initial window + reserve; min(2.3 MB, ceiling); ceiling | every connection's QUIC flow control | least_transport | ARBITRARY | high | inherits least_transport (12 kB). Replace: as least_transport |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:105` | `Plan::standard span` | 256 MiB (≤ a tenth of free space) | the calibration's scratch file | — | ARBITRARY | medium | picked; a span within the device's cache measures the cache. Replace: a span past the device's measured write cache (detect by the throughput step as the span grows), within free space |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:107` | `Plan::standard step` | 500 ms | a round's length | — | ARBITRARY | medium | a time, where measurement.md §5a decides 'a round is a count of transfers, not a length of time'. Replace: transfers a round from the quantile and cv |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:108` | `Plan::standard random_depths; next_depth ×4` | [1, 4, 16, 64], then ×4 | read depths measured; the issuer's depth is chosen among them | — | ARBITRARY | medium | a ladder ratio picked; the knee lands only on powers of four. Replace: bisect between the last growing and the first flat depth to the depth whose step is inside the interval |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:111` | `Plan::standard sequential_depths` | [1, 4] | sequential depths measured | — | ARBITRARY | medium | picked. Replace: the same growing ladder as random reads |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:113` | `Plan::standard large` | 1 MiB | the sequential transfer measured | — | ARBITRARY | medium | picked. Replace: grow the transfer until throughput stops growing past its interval |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:114` | `Plan::standard durable_writes` | 64 | durable writes measured | constants.md: 64 support a median, not a p99 | ARBITRARY | medium | picked. Replace: Le Boudec's count for the quantile used |
| `crates/mantle/src/bench.rs:120-121` | `bench chunk sizes, workers` | 4K, 64K, 1M, 8M; 1, 4, 16, 64 | the points measured | — | ARBITRARY | medium | picked. Replace: sizes the node derives (block, chunk, segment), workers by a ladder to the measured knee |
| `crates/mantle/src/bench_doors.rs:2732` | `ladder step` | clients × 2 | the closed ladder's next step | — | ARBITRARY | medium | doubling chosen. Replace: grow by the measured gain (stop where the next step's interval overlaps), bisect to the knee |
| `crates/mantle/src/bench_log.rs:67-72` | `bench log sizes, replicas` | 128, 1K, 16K; 1, 4, 16, 64, 256 | the points measured | — | ARBITRARY | medium | picked. Replace: the derived entry sizes (least and largest entry) and replicas by ladder |
| `crates/mantle/src/main.rs:273-385` | `bench --seconds defaults` | 1.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 2.0, 0.5 | how long each measurement of chunk, log, ec, gateway, hash, http, s3, meta runs | — | ARBITRARY | medium | durations, where measurement.md §5a decides rounds are counts of transfers. Replace: transfers from the quantile and cv (measurement.md §5a) |
| `crates/node/src/derive.rs:469` | `Engine.memtable_bytes` | entry_bytes(max key, max value): one row at the bounds | an engine's write buffer before it packs | 'the least write buffer, one row at the bounds whole' | ARBITRARY | medium | a floor: the memtable holds about one largest row (≈ 20 KB, about a dozen small objects' rows at the 1,841 store bytes an object measured) before it packs into the trunk; cost not measured (engine and range were 18–34% of a 1 B PUT's instructions, research/71). Replace: size the write buffer against the cache by measured marginal savings (the engine's own Luo and Carey tuner, PVLDB 2021), within the share |
| `crates/protocol/src/bounds.rs:201` | `Limits.lanes_per_peer` | 1 | Raft lanes a peer opens | 'the node runs its ranges on one shard' | ARBITRARY | medium | inherits shards = 1. Replace: a lane a shard once shards are derived |
| `vendor/hyper-quic/src/config/transport.rs:581` | `AckFrequencyConfig::decimation` | ACK every 10 packets, RTT/4, from the 100th packet | acknowledgement frequency asked of peers | Google QUIC's constants (quiche) | ARBITRARY | medium | another implementation's tuning. Replace: ACK frequency from the measured cost per ACK and the sender's need (draft-ietf-quic-ack-frequency leaves the threshold and delay to the sender), from the measured cost of an ACK against its measured effect on loss detection |
| `crates/chunk/src/layout.rs:224` | `Config::default` | segment 256 MiB, checksum_shift 16, max_fragments 2^22, scrub 7 days | the chunk bench's volume | — | ARBITRARY | low | bench only: picked. Replace: derive::volume on the measured device |
| `crates/chunk/src/layout.rs:56` | `least_segment` | ≥ 4 blocks | the least segment | 'four of them at least' | ARBITRARY | low | unexplained. Replace: the header block, a record's least and its table (structural) |

| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:115` | `Plan::standard durable_large` | 32 MiB | the large durable write measured | — | ARBITRARY | low | picked. Replace: the volume's segment, the write it makes |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs:315` | `span ≤ free / 10` | a tenth | calibration's share of free space | — | ARBITRARY | low | picked. Replace: the span the measurement needs, refused when free space past the node's reserve cannot hold it |
| `crates/mantle/src/bench.rs:339-347` | `chunk bench index` | a third of the volume in the smallest chunks, twice over, at least 1,024 | the bench volume's index | — | ARBITRARY | low | picked. Replace: derive::index_wants |
| `crates/mantle/src/bench_s3.rs:180` | `bench s3 runtime` | step_budget 1 s, timer_tick 100 µs | the s3 bench's runtime | — | ARBITRARY | low | as the doors harness |
| `crates/mantle/src/main.rs:290` | `bench chunk --rounds` | 30 (rounds::Policy::STANDARD.max) | rounds a point runs at most | research/21 §9.5 ('a fifth') | ARBITRARY | low | inherits rounds STANDARD |
| `crates/mantle/src/main.rs:421` | `bench transfer --volume` | 256M | each founder's volume | — | ARBITRARY | low | picked. Replace: --volume free less the reserve (its own option) |
| `crates/mantle/src/main.rs:881-922` | `bench ec/gateway/hash sizes` | 64K,1M,8M; 64K,8M,64M; 8K,64K,1M,8M | the points measured | — | ARBITRARY | low | picked. Replace: the derived chunk/segment/block sizes |
| `crates/node/src/derive.rs:175` | `Gateway.scan_rows` | 1 | rows a bucket coordinator's read or collection passes at a step | 'the least that moves it' | ARBITRARY | low | a floor; bucket create/delete pass one row a command round. Replace: rows a step = what one entry of the range's largest bytes holds (derive from row bounds), or the measured rows a commit round amortizes |
| `crates/node/src/process.rs:1677` | `Policy.reserved_cores / latency_objective_ns` | 0 / None | what the runtime's calibration reserves and targets | none stated | ARBITRARY | low | no latency objective is given, so the runtime cannot derive its spin and park choices from the node's tail target. ## 7. Limits of this audit

- The verdicts are judgements against the rule, made from each number's code, comment and
  constants.md row. Mantle's cited, measured, bound and open rows, every unlisted array or struct,
  every literal parameter above and every vendored number the auto-tagging marked as copied or
  could not place were judged one by one (the tables in `/tmp/constaudit/overrides_*.py` and
  `literals_data.py`). The rest were tagged from their kind (format → derived; external →
  well-researched; derived → derived unless an input is arbitrary) or, in vendor/, from what their
  comment cites (an RFC, a header, a paper: well-researched; a field width, a flag, a unit: derived),
  and spot-checked. Test constants were judged by pattern (a frozen configuration, a wait, a count,
  an identifier, a fixture), with every non-engine test constant reviewed one by one; appendix B lists
  every one under its pattern.
- Literal parameters were searched for on the write path and in the bench; a literal in a
  production function off the write path (the S3 layer's parsers, the engine's codecs) was not
  hunted one by one, and the engine's 65,687 lines were read through constants.md and their `const`
  items only.
- Nothing was run. Impacts marked "not measured" are the code's reading of what the number costs,
  not a measurement of it; the flush and concurrency arithmetic of section 1 uses research/71's
  measurements as they stand.

## Appendix A. Every constant, production and bench

A row each for the arbitrary ones; the rest grouped by file, each with its line, name, value and,
in brackets, what it decides (its comment's first clause). "Basis claimed" is constants.md's kind and row where there is one, else the constant's comment.

## Sources the replacements cite

- Little, J. D. C. "A Proof for the Queuing Formula: L = λW." *Operations Research* 9(3), 1961.
- Brakmo, L. S., and Peterson, L. L. "TCP Vegas: New Techniques for Congestion Detection and
  Avoidance." SIGCOMM 1994.
- Cho, I., Saeed, A., Fried, J., Park, S. J., Alizadeh, M., and Belay, A. "Overload Control for
  µs-scale RPCs with Breakwater." OSDI 2020.
- Ongaro, D. *Consensus: Bridging Theory and Practice.* PhD thesis, Stanford, 2014: §6.3
  (sessions), §10.2 (batching and pipelining).
- Santos, N., and Schiper, A. "Tuning Paxos for High-Throughput with Batching and Pipelining."
  ICDCN 2012.
- Helland, P., Sammer, H., Lyon, J., Carr, R., Garrett, P., and Reuter, A. "Group Commit Timers
  and High Volume Transaction Systems." Tandem Technical Report 88.1, 1988, §2.
- DeWitt, D. J., Katz, R. H., Olken, F., Shapiro, L. D., Stonebraker, M. R., and Wood, D.
  "Implementation Techniques for Main Memory Database Systems." SIGMOD 1984, §5.2.
- Iyer, S., and Druschel, P. "Anticipatory Scheduling: A Disk Scheduling Framework to Overcome
  Deceptive Idleness in Synchronous I/O." SOSP 2001, §3.3.
- Kleinrock, L. "Power and Deterministic Rules of Thumb for Probabilistic Problems in Computer
  Communications." ICC 1979.
- Cardwell, N., Cheng, Y., Gunn, C. S., Yeganeh, S. H., and Jacobson, V. "BBR: Congestion-Based
  Congestion Control." *ACM Queue* 14(5), 2016.
- Semke, J., Mahdavi, J., and Mathis, M. "Automatic TCP Buffer Tuning." SIGCOMM 1998.
- Fisk, M., and Feng, W. "Dynamic Right-Sizing in TCP." Los Alamos Computer Science Institute
  Symposium, 2001.
- RFC 9000 (QUIC) §4, §10.1; RFC 9002 (loss detection and congestion control) §5–§7; RFC 6298
  (retransmission timer) §2, §5; RFC 9110 §10.2.3 (`Retry-After`); draft-ietf-quic-ack-frequency.
- Schroeder, B., Wierman, A., and Harchol-Balter, M. "Open Versus Closed: A Cautionary Tale."
  NSDI 2006.
- Le Boudec, J.-Y. *Performance Evaluation of Computer and Communication Systems.* EPFL Press,
  2010, Theorem 2.1. Jain, R. *The Art of Computer Systems Performance Analysis.* Wiley, 1991.

  Evaluation." OOPSLA 2007.

- Dayan, N., Athanassoulis, M., and Idreos, S. "Monkey: Optimal Navigable Key-Value Store."
